Outage Filename: ./20190823-Summary of the Amazon EC2 Issues in the Asia Pacific (Tokyo) Region (AP-NORTHEAST-1).txt
--
After analyzing the text, I found that only Amazon AWS was mentioned as a cloud provider. Here is the information about the outage:

Amazon AWS

 Service: EC2
 Region/Availability Zone: Tokyo (AP-NORTHEAST-1)
 Impact Date: August 23, 2019, 12:36 PM JST
 Duration:
	 EC2 instances and degraded EBS volume performance for some resources in the affected area of the Availability Zone: 5 hours and 30 minutes
	 EC2 RunInstances API: 1 hour and 21 minutes
 Root Cause: Control system failure that caused multiple, redundant cooling systems to fail in parts of the affected Availability Zone.
 Downtime Period:
	 EC2 instances and degraded EBS volume performance for some resources in the affected area of the Availability Zone: 5 hours and 30 minutes
	 EC2 RunInstances API: 1 hour and 21 minutes
 Resolved Date: August 23, 2019, 6:30 PM JST

Summary

Amazon AWS experienced an outage on August 23, 2019, affecting EC2 instances and degraded EBS volume performance in the Tokyo (AP-NORTHEAST-1) Region. The root cause was a control system failure that caused multiple cooling systems to fail. The outage lasted for approximately 6 hours and 51 minutes.

Lambda

Overall Summary

The outage impacted multiple services across Azure in China, including Cosmos DB, Azure Virtual Desktop, Azure Databricks, Azure IoT Hub, Azure Policy, Azure Kubernetes Services, Azure Service Bus, Azure Logic Apps, Azure Data Factory, and Azure AI Speech Services. The outage occurred between 03:45 CST and 06:00 CST on April 23, 2024, and lasted for approximately 2 hours and 15 minutes.

Root Cause

The root cause of the outage was a configuration change through a domain registrar that resulted in a service disruption.

Recommendations

To prevent similar outages in the future, it is recommended to:

 Implement additional validations to obtain nameserver verification by directly resolving the zone over internet
 Ensure that the right people in your organization will be notified about any future service issues - by configuring Azure Service Health alerts

Note: The outage was resolved on April 23, 2024, at 05:13 CST.

 Region/Availability Zone: us-central1
 Impact Date: 2024-09-04 06:29 US/Pacific Time

Overall Summary

A global outage occurred on September 4, 2024, affecting multiple Google Cloud services. The root cause was a regressive change in the rollout strategy, which caused all incoming requests to be discarded and resulted in widespread RPC errors. The outage affected several regions and availability zones globally, with the longest downtime period reported for Google App Engine Flexible. The outage was resolved at 13:24 US/Pacific on September 4, 2024.

Here is the information about the outage:

Google Cloud

The incident began on October 23, 2024, at 18:22 US/Pacific Time and ended on October 24, 2024, at 02:01 US/Pacific Time.

The impacted services were:
- Google Cloud Dataflow
- Google Cloud Dataproc
- Google Cloud Pub/Sub
- Vertex AI Batch Prediction
- Cloud SQL

The root cause of the outage was a misconfiguration and triggered by the outage in europe-west3-c. To prevent a recurrence of this issue, engineers have hardened this component against zonal outages in europe-west3.

The downtime period for each service was:
- Google Cloud Dataflow: 19:05 US/Pacific Time to 23:55 US/Pacific Time (6 hours and 50 minutes)
- Google Cloud Dataproc: 19:12 US/Pacific Time to 22:00 US/Pacific Time (2 hours and 48 minutes)
- Google Cloud Pub/Sub: 18:25 US/Pacific Time to 18:41 US/Pacific Time (16 minutes) and elevated latency for push subscriptions from 22:57 US/Pacific Time to 00:11 US/Pacific Time (1 hour and 14 minutes)
- Vertex AI Batch Prediction: 19:45 US/Pacific Time to 23:30 US/Pacific Time (3 hours and 45 minutes)
- Cloud SQL: 18:35 US/Pacific Time to 24 October 2024, 23:28 US/Pacific Time (5 hours and 53 minutes)

The outage was marked as resolved on October 24, 2024.

Summary

Google Cloud was impacted by an outage that began on October 23, 2024, at 18:22 US/Pacific Time and ended on October 24, 2024, at 02:01 US/Pacific Time. The services affected were Google Cloud Dataflow, Google Cloud Dataproc, Google Cloud Pub/Sub, Vertex AI Batch Prediction, and Cloud SQL.

Outage Filename: ./Google Cloud Service Health - Incident report 2024-11-04.txt
--
Here is the information about the outage:

Summary

The outage affected Google Cloud Service Health, specifically Vertex AI Online Prediction (Vertex Model Garden Deployments) in most regions except asia-southeast1, europe-west4, us-central1, us-east1, and us-east4.

Outage Details

Google Cloud

 Service: Large Vertex Model Garden Deployment
 Region/Availability Zone: All regions except asia-southeast1, europe-west4, us-central1, us-east1, and us-east4
 Impact Date: 2024-10-22 14:08 US/Pacific
 Duration: 13 days, 6 hours, 22 minutes
 Root Cause: Internal storage provisioning configuration error
 Downtime Period: Model server never became ready during deployments
 Resolved Date: 2024-11-04 20:30 US/Pacific

Incident Report

Mini Incident Report:

We apologize for the inconvenience this service disruption/outage may have caused. We would like to provide some information about this incident below.

 Incident Start: 22 October 2024, 14:08
 Duration: 13 days, 6 hours, 22 minutes
 Affecting Services and Features: Vertex AI Online Prediction (Vertex Model Garden Deployments)
 Regions/Zones: All regions except asia-southeast1, europe-west4, us-central1, us-east1, us-east4
 Description:
Deployment of large models (those that require more than 100GB of disk size) in Vertex AI Online Prediction (Vertex Model Garden Deployments) failed in most of the regions for a duration of up to 13 days, 6 hours, 22 minutes starting on Tuesday, 22 October 2024 at 14:08 US/Pacific.
From preliminary analysis, the root cause of the issue is an internal storage provisioning configuration error that was implemented as part of a recent change.

Workaround

As a workaround, customers were able to deploy in one of the non-impacted regions noted above.

Summary of Outage

The outage started on October 22, 2024, at 14:08 US/Pacific and lasted for 13 days, 6 hours, 22 minutes. The root cause was an internal storage provisioning configuration error. The downtime period was during deployments when customers received errors stating "Model server never became ready". The outage was resolved on November 4, 2024, at 20:30 US/Pacific.

Overall Summary

The outage affected IBM Cloud's WDC01 data center, specifically Pod05, on 06 April 2021 at 08:17. The outage was caused by a failure of the automated power switching logic due to a failure of the dedicated UPS. This resulted in critical load not being transferred from UPS to generator, leading to a partial power outage within the data center.

The outage impacted various services, including Cloudant NoSQL DB, VSI-San Storage, Load Balancers, and API Connect, which were partially impacted during power restoration.

IBM Cloud

 Services impacted: Private DNS, HyperProtect-VS, MqCloud, Mobile Foundation, Hyper Protect DBaaS
 Regions/Availability Zones: Frankfurt, London, Tokyo, Sydney, US-East, US-South
 Impact Date: 25 May 2021 13:33 UTC
 Duration: 12 hours 7 minutes
 Root Cause: Resource constraints in Cloudant HA Proxy Load Balancers caused by increased database queries from Private DNS
 Downtime Period: 25 May 2021 01:40 - 26 May 2021 01:40 UTC
 Resolved Date: 26 May 2021 01:40 UTC

Sequence of Events

 25 May 2021 13:33 - IBM Cloudant Specialists received alerts from multiple system areas indicating timeouts and connection loss. Incident started.
 25 May 2021 14:20 - Affected clusters were identified.
 25 May 2021 15:16 - Disruptive traffic was identified. Isolation activity began for affected clusters.
 25 May 2021 15:51 - Isolation and removal of Private DNS traffic allowed cluster traffic to restart in Sydney.
 25 May 2021 16:09 - Service recovery in Sydney was confirmed and isolation of traffic continued for other clusters.
 26 May 2021 01:28 - Rate-limited updates were rolled out across all affected regions. Limited service resumed.
 26 May 2021 01:40 - Cloudant services returned to normal state. Incident ended.

Summary

The outage impacted IBM Cloud services in multiple regions and availability zones, including Frankfurt, London, Tokyo, Sydney, US-East, and US-South. The root cause of the outage was resource constraints in Cloudant HA Proxy Load Balancers caused by increased database queries from Private DNS. The outage lasted for 12 hours and 7 minutes, with a downtime period of 12 hours and 7 minutes.

Summary of Outage

IBM Cloud was impacted by a utility grid outage that started on 20 June 2023 03:49 UTC and lasted for 18 hours 26 minutes. The root cause of the outage was a failed relay on the generator control system resulting in a soft (partial) failure scenario that impeded handover between the UPS (battery) and generator systems.

The impacted services were:

 Watson platform
 VPC
 Load Balancer
 Network Infrastructure
 VSI
 SQL Query
 Databases

Overall Summary

IBM Cloud was impacted by a utility grid outage that started on 20 June 2023 03:49 UTC and lasted for 18 hours 26 minutes. The root cause of the outage was a failed relay on the generator control system resulting in a soft (partial) failure scenario that impeded handover between the UPS (battery) and generator systems. The impacted services were affected by the outage, which resulted in degraded performance and delays in creating or updating applications.

After analyzing the text, I found that only IBM Cloud was mentioned as a cloud provider. Here is the information about the outage:

IBM Cloud

The outage started on June 20, 2023, at 03:49 UTC and lasted for 18 hours and 26 minutes.

Here are the services impacted by the outage, listed one by one:

 VPC underlying host operating system was restored.
 All bare metal hosts were back online.
 Power was restored on the affected rack.
 Facilities grid power supply was restored and transition from generator to utility was completed.
 IBM Cloud Site Operator Engineering team were notified that power was restored after inspection.
 VPC SDN was restored.
 SQL Query - SQL jobs/queries experienced long delays and eventually failures for all submitted jobs. Streaming jobs were running as normal where the impact was limited to SQL batch jobs.
 Databases - IBM Cloud Databases had an impact due to worker nodes being in critical status.
 Load Balancer - Data plane and control plane for Load Balancers were impacted.
 Network Infrastructure - All IBM Cloud services experienced connectivity issues.
 VPC VPN - Impacted customers experienced VPN Gateway provisioning failures and VSI was stuck in "pending" status.
 Code Engine - Impacted customers experienced delays in creating or updating applications, due to the degraded performance of the system.
 Watson platform - Impacted customers experienced intermittent 5xx errors when using the following Watson AI services: 
Watson Assistant
Discovery
Natural Language Understanding
Language Translator
Knowledge Studio

IBM Cloud

Summary of Outage

The outage affected multiple IBM Cloud services in all regions. The third-party vendor's BGP configuration change caused a critical set of IP prefixes to be withdrawn, leading to a global outage on their PoPs. The outage was resolved after the vendor reverted all changes.

The previous outages affected Amazon Elastic Compute Cloud in the ap-northeast-2 region (3 hours and 18 minutes), AWS Billing Console (1 hour and 32 minutes), AWS Partner Central (1 hour and 12 minutes), and AWS QuickSight (2 hours and 28 minutes).

The root cause of the outages was not specified in any of the cases.
